  In new worlds, import tariffs now apply to every cross-border sale in the producer market. Buyers see the price including the tariff, and the money goes to the importing country's treasury. Existing worlds are unchanged until switched on.
- **The 2050s now begin on schedule, and the last research tier no longer shows a stale date range**
  Games that reach 2050 now get the 2050s decade announcement, and the final research tier is shown as open-ended instead of ending in 2039.
- **Turn processing spends less time sharing scarce inputs between buyers with route limits**
  Working out which suppliers each buyer can reach when scarce inputs are shared fairly is now done once per group instead of at every price level. Results are unchanged.
- **Turns no longer skip the economy after a mid-turn restart**
  If the turn engine is restarted while company profits are being calculated, the turn now stops and waits for a repair instead of finishing without them.
- **Two new settings to get idle plants running**
  Two settings, unchanged until they are set: how much of its expected sales a company can borrow against to run with no cash, and how long a bankrupt AI company keeps its plants before they pass to a funded owner.
- **A finished market is no longer thrown away at the last step**
  When the market had already been worked out but the time limit ran out while saving the result, the turn failed and the same work was redone. The result is now saved instead.
- **AI companies keep a smaller cash buffer before they expand**
  Computer-run companies now hold a smaller minimum cash reserve, so more of them can expand into goods that stay in short supply.
- **Background country economy fixes**
  In a 2027 world, shocks now cut a background country's tax base and national index as well as its market supply, national index funds stay fully backed when you buy while they reprice, and foreign policy decisions are no longer lost when they fail to take effect.
- **Corporation founding moved into its own tested module**
  The steps that found a new corporation now live in one place with their own tests. Founding works exactly as before.
- **Court reversal recovery no longer double-credits or loses money**
  Two remaining gaps in recovering compensation after a Supreme Court reversal are closed: an interruption can no longer credit the treasury twice, and a spun-out company is covered from the moment the asset starts moving.
- **Court reversals recover compensation reliably**
  When the Supreme Court strikes down an emergency taking, the compensation the government paid is now recovered even for whole-company takings, for sectors taken more than once, and after an interruption mid-recovery.
- **Era progression, sector tech trees and the Cold War are now always on**
  Era progression, sector technology trees and the Cold War systems no longer depend on a world setting. They were already on in the live world and in every new world.
- **Every bondholder is paid when a defaulted company restructures or closes**
  Index funds, autonomous parties and the public bond market now get their share when a company restructures, closes or refinances defaulted bonds. State-owned companies pay their own bond interest.
- **Fewer lost payouts in caucus tax, share splits and buyouts**
  Closes several gaps where money or shares could vanish: caucus tax after a failed deposit, party-held stakes in share splits and company wind-ups, and a double refund when cancelling a take-private vote.
- **Government debt walls stay small**
  When many government bonds mature on the same turn, they are now always refinanced into one shared bond per country, coupon and turn, even in a world that never stored the setting. Debt, interest and holder balances are unchanged.
- **Income tax is only taken from income you received**
  Savings interest, bank interest, dividends, coupons and salary are taxed before they reach you, so no tax bill is left against money you never held. New checks cover three years of turns with empty wallets.
- **Inflation no longer inflates real capital**
  Investment is now measured in real terms before it builds a region's capital stock, so rising prices alone no longer make an economy richer.
- **Late breakthroughs depend on other industries**
  Space Resources and Autonomous Districts now stay closed until the industries they rely on have done enough late-tier research.
- **Market step no longer stops a turn early**
  The market step's time limit now follows the turn's phase limit, so a large but normal market is no longer abandoned and the turn can advance.
- **Own-site market-value booking reaches companies without a transfer price**
  The opt-in flag for booking own-site deliveries at market value was never set, so that booking wrote nothing for any company. Every company except one on Free transfer pricing now opts in.
- **Plants selling at a loss cut back instead of running until the money runs out**
  Behind the next step of the production planning switch, a plant that sells everything it makes but below what it costs to run now makes less each turn, instead of running flat out until its company has no cash left.
- **Plants with a pile of unsold goods make less until it clears**
  Behind the next step of the production planning switch, a plant's guaranteed minimum run no longer counts stock it is already holding beyond a turn's sales, so a plant sitting on a pile sells from it instead of making more.
- **Preserve Scotland and Wales economic accounts at independence**
  Independence carries regional cash, invoices, funding and resource capacity into the successor regions, and their regional budgets continue processing.
- **Sector run card counts unsold units in its per-unit profit**
  The "Earns a unit" figure on a sector's run card now spreads the whole bill over the units that sold, so it matches the profit shown in the sector's financials when part of the output goes unsold.
- **Take-private buyouts no longer pay funds and then fail**
  A company held by an index fund and a political party could pay the fund and then refuse to go private. It now goes to a vote instead. A stuck contract award is also released when the winning company disappears.
- **The market plans production more carefully each turn**
  The market gets more time to settle how much each plant should make, so production plans swing less from one turn to the next.
- **A refused construction instalment no longer freezes the lender's whole construction book**
  When one borrower could not cover a construction instalment, the lender's other construction loans stayed locked and sales of financed sectors were refused. The batch now completes without that one payment.
- **Any company with an extraction sector can run surveys there**
  A company that operates an extraction sector in a state can now launch a geological survey in that state, whatever its main or secondary business.
- **Clearer warning when building below the cash reserve**
  The build warning now says which plants low cash can slow: lines that do not pay for themselves and new lines with no sales yet.
- **Commodity page supply now says it includes unsold stock**
  The supply figure at the top of a commodity page is units offered for sale, including stock that did not sell earlier. It is now labelled that way.
- **Faster turns: fewer database round trips for index funds and the Treasury**
  Index fund NAV updates and the Treasury budget check now go to the database in batches instead of one call per fund or country. Prices and money are unchanged.
- **Founding and charter fees reach spendable Treasury cash**
  Fees paid to a country for spin-offs, low-confidence company founding and index fund charters now add to the Treasury's spendable cash, not just its headline balance.
- **Government fines are paid straight into Treasury cash**
  Overdue merger divestiture fines and transfer pricing assessments now move money from the company to the national Treasury in one step, so the turn no longer stalls on them.
- **Privatization sale proceeds reach Treasury cash reliably**
  Selling off a state-owned company, by share offering or by auction, now pays the proceeds into spendable Treasury cash in one step instead of failing.
- **Resolve My Corporation when opened**
- **Run panel no longer blames a small input shortage for large idle capacity**
  The sector run panel now credits short inputs only with the output they actually cut from the plant's own target, instead of the whole gap to capacity.
- **Selling a national index fund at a moving price**
  Selling units of a background country's national index fund while it is being repriced is now refused with a request to try again, the same as buying.
- **Share scarce household supply fairly across countries**
- **Short inputs are marked on the sector page**
  When part of a sector's run stops for lack of an input, the Inputs list now marks the input that ran short and links to the companies selling it.
- **Stop the Soviet debt arrears total from overflowing**
  The running total of unpaid successor contributions to inherited Soviet debt now counts each missed payment once, so it no longer grows every turn and stops turns.
- **The Prospect button only shows for companies that can prospect**
  Companies that are not extraction companies no longer see a Prospect button on extraction sectors, and the refusal message now says what is actually required.
- **Use funded orders and correct market scopes in build advice**
- **Central banks lean against inflation in the long-run fiscal tables**
  The offline long-run budget tables now set each central bank's rate from its neutral rate plus the inflation gap, instead of copying the neutral rate. No change to the live game or to the published 2027 tables.
- **Corporation records get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of corporation records now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Every new research method now lists its build time and its downside**
  The methods opened by the 2039 and 2049 research tiers each show what they need, how long a site takes to build, and what they cost you beyond inputs.
- **Frontier race methods now add real output, and unpaid points cannot win**
  A held frontier method now adds its output bonus on top of the normal tech cap instead of being swallowed by it, and points that have not been paid for yet cannot make an entrant the winner.
- **Global crises count in leader legacy, briefs find every country's bond, subsidised pay is not shown as lower**
  Worldwide crises now count towards a departing leader's legacy, background country briefs always find each country's nearest bond, and workers under the hiring subsidy are no longer shown as paid less.
- **Income tax tiers keep up with prices**
  The low, medium and high income tax tiers for players now move with the price level. Earning the same real income as prices rise no longer pushes a player into a higher tier.
- **Moderator ledger timeline is now in turn order**
  The moderator and admin financial ledger lists transactions by turn, newest first, instead of by when each log entry happened to be written.
- **Money tables get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of national budgets, bonds, central banks and the financial transaction log now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Net worth counts shares your companies hold in other companies**
  A company's shares in another company now count toward the net worth of its shareholders, in proportion to their stake.
- **New 2039 methods take time to build and carry a cost**
  The 2039 methods for finance, retail, vehicles, real estate and mining now take years to set up and each has a clear drawback.
- **New production methods take time to build and carry a one-off cost**
  Switching to a late-game method now keeps the old method running until the new one is built, and adds the method's downside to the switching bill.
- **Plant labour cost follows the funded run**
  A plant running far below its crew share no longer shows an inflated labour cost. The figure now matches the wages actually paid, which also corrects the base for union pension charges.
- **Plants stuck at a minimal run can grow again**
  When a market is only a few percent oversupplied, plants that had dropped to their minimum run now step back up a little each turn instead of staying stuck.
- **Sector run panel no longer blames inputs for a stop it cannot explain**
  When a sector stops short of capacity and the records show no input or cash cut, the idle-capacity breakdown now lists the gap under other limits.
- **Automated methods cut crews only once they are built**
  A sector waiting for an automated method to be built keeps its full crew and pays no automation levy until the new method is running.
- **Japan's 1991 opening budget balances again**
  In new 1991 worlds Japan's law book programs are sized to its authored opening budget, the same way the United States and the United Kingdom are, so Japan no longer opens with a large surplus.
- **Merger review uses the new competition laws in Germany, Japan and China**
  Germany, Japan and China now review mergers under the competition law in their new law books, like the other law-book countries. Two automated checks that had stopped seeing what they guard work again.
- **Net worth history counts shares your companies hold in other companies**
  The net worth shown in the sidebar, dashboard and wealth history now matches the wealth list for players whose companies own shares in other companies.
- **No Bank of Canada in worlds before Canada joins**
  New worlds from eras before 2027 no longer create a Bank of Canada for a country that is not part of that world. The Canadian dollar keeps its exchange rate for valuations.
- **Opening national budgets for 1953 and 1979 live in each country's folder**
  The authored 1953 and 1979 national budgets moved out of one giant file into each country's own folder. The numbers are unchanged.
- **The My Corporation menu link opens your company again**
  The My Corporation link in the World menu could send players to an internal server address that does not load. It now takes them straight to their company page.
- **The switch price includes a method's downside charge and build time**
  The strategy picker now quotes the full price of switching to a late-game method and says how long the old method keeps running while it is built.
- **The company step of a turn has ten minutes again**
  The company step of a turn is allowed ten minutes, so turns that need a little over six minutes no longer record a timeout warning.
- **Turns no longer stop because the market takes a little longer**
  The company step of a turn now has ten minutes instead of five, so a large market finishes and the turn moves on instead of failing and retrying.

### Interface and platform

- **Ask Keir answer length: Concise or Standard**
  The Ask drawer now has a Concise / Standard choice beside the mode picker, the same one the Ask site has.
- **Ask Keir recovers when the connection drops mid-answer**
  If your connection drops or stalls while Ask is researching, the drawer now shows "Reconnecting..." and picks the finished answer up instead of freezing.
- **New read-only staff role for junior developers**
  Admins can now give an account the junior dev role. It sees everything an admin sees, including logs and diagnostics, but cannot change anything.
- **One control panel for admins, moderators and junior devs**
  The separate admin and moderator panels are now a single control panel. Each role sees only the tools it can use, grouped by area.
- **The wiki explains what Canada loses when Quebec leaves**
  The Canada and Referendums wiki pages now say how many seats Canada's Parliament keeps after Quebec secedes and how the federal debt is shared.
- **The wiki has a Canada page**
  A new Canada page in the wiki covers the House of Commons, forming a government, the provinces, Quebec's sovereignty mood, the parties and money.
- **Ask drawer research spinner is a ring again**
  While Ask is researching, the progress spinner around the step card turns as a circle instead of tumbling as a tilted square.
- **Ask Keir research row is one tidy line**
  While Ask researches, the progress card is now a single row with the current step and a "Show N" link, like the Ask site.
- **Single player autosaves keep their spacing on fast turns**
  Autosaves no longer come a turn early when several turns finish within the same instant.
- **Lighter pages for region conditions, corporations and campaigns**
  Several pages no longer download server-side game data they never use, so they load less code in your browser. Nothing you see or do changes.
- **The 2027 world splits countries into player, NPP-run and background tiers**
  In a new 2027 world, the United States is the player country, the United Kingdom, Germany, Japan and China run in full under NPPs, and sixteen other countries become background countries that skip the full simulation. Other start dates and existing worlds are unchanged.
- **Bell notifications open the item you clicked**
  Clicking a notification in the bell dropdown now opens the inbox with that item selected, and the mark read and delete buttons show on touch screens.
- **Conflicts and five more systems are now always on**
  The Conflicts pages, character stats, bloc alignment, subsidiary corporations, US House redistricting and detailed demographic positions are no longer behind an admin switch. Single-player setup no longer lists them as optional.
- **Inbox Mark all read works on long lists**
  Mark all read now clears a long unread list in a couple of requests instead of one per item, so big inboxes no longer fail to clear.
- **Opening an inbox item no longer jumps to another one**
  An unread inbox item now stays open on the list after you click it, instead of dropping out and showing a different message.
- **Pause Government Confidence while it is reworked**
  An Admin switch freezes Government Confidence at its current value across countries.
- **Retired feature switches that were always on**
  Fifteen game systems that every world already ran are no longer behind an admin switch. Nothing changes for players in the live world; single-player setup no longer lists them as optional.
- **Retired more feature switches that were always on**
  Nine more game systems that every world already ran are no longer behind an admin switch. Nothing changes for players in the live world; single-player setup no longer lists them as optional.
- **Shared access to the busiest game records**
  Behind the scenes, the turn now reads and writes characters, corporations, regions, officials, parties, elections and budgets through one shared, typed entry point. Nothing changes in play.
- **Single player statistics accept reports from older versions**
  Statistics from older single player versions are no longer rejected because they mention settings that have since been retired.
- **Voter-group counts removed from the state demographics tab**
  The state demographics tab no longer shows a count of legacy voter-group categories, and the Commons carve-up note now describes the split by demographic affinity only.
- **Code hygiene counts can no longer grow unnoticed**
  New developer checks stop console calls, silent empty catch blocks, lint suppressions, explicit any types and one-line re-export files from growing.
- **Crisis turn reads the region list once**
  The crisis step now reuses the region list the crisis watchers already load each turn. No gameplay changes.
- **Faster producer market step in the hourly turn**
  The producer market part of the corporation turn does less repeated work when it clears orders and saves its receipt. Results are unchanged.
- **Faster turns with crisis detection on**
  The crisis watchers now share one database read each turn instead of making one each, which trims turn time. No gameplay changes.
- **Position editor previews the census electorate**
  The admin position editor now previews each region from the census buckets the vote engine counts, and the retired voter-group category data is gone.
- **Region demographics tab covers every country**
  The demographics tab on region pages no longer depends on a per-country voter-group profile. Every country with census data shows its electorate.
- **Seeded region leans come from the census**
  Every country seed now sets a region's starting lean from its census electorate and no longer stores old voter-group rows.
- **Architecture check allowances tightened to current levels**
  Several developer checks kept a little unused room for new debt. Their recorded limits now match what the code actually contains.
- **iPhone alerts can open your inbox directly**
  Game alerts on iPhone and iPad carry the app's alert category, so the app can offer an "Open inbox" button right on the notification.
- **Lighter pages for country and corporation screens**
  Flag and state lists now load from small files, so several screens download less code.
- **Pages import currency symbols from a small module**
  Screens that only show a currency symbol or a country's currency no longer pull the exchange rate tables into the browser download.
- **Singleplayer downloads build again for Apple Silicon Macs**
  The singleplayer game release builds its Apple Silicon Mac package on a supported build machine again, so every platform's download is published.
- **Singleplayer reports what this game version can start**
  The local game now publishes which eras, countries, modes and options it supports, so the desktop app shows only choices that will work.
- **Singleplayer says plainly when an era cannot be started yet**
  Starting a singleplayer world in an era that is still being rebuilt now gives a short, clear message instead of failing partway through setup.
- **Starting a new world with a missing or unknown scenario now stops with a clear error**
  World setup, reset and the 2027 pre-launch check refuse a missing or unrecognised scenario before changing anything. Live worlds are unaffected.
- **US state list moved into the US folder**
  Internal tidy-up of where the list of US state codes lives.
- **World setup stops instead of guessing a missing era**
  If a world has no era recorded, setup now stops with a clear message instead of quietly building a 1953 or 2019 world.
- **Background governments decide cyber operations with fewer reads**
  Turns spend less time on background governments' cyber decisions. Nothing about what they decide changes.
- **Ledger ordering index is now checked on every start**
  The index that keeps the ledger page fast is now created or confirmed each time the game starts, instead of only when someone runs it by hand.
- **Release checks accept the reviewed reset scan change**
  The check that tracks which collections the post-reset scan reads now knows about the scan's new retired law check. No change for players.
- **Single player launch check expects Canada as a 2027 player country**
  The automated single player launch check now expects the 2027 world to offer both the United States and Canada. The game itself is unchanged.
- **The reset preflight says why an economy reset will be refused**
  When a world still holds laws from the old law book, the pre-reset check now tells the admin that only a full world reset clears them.
- **Wiki charts no longer download the game engine into the browser**
  A small helper import pulled the turn engine, budgets and law books into the browser bundle behind wiki charts. Pages that show those charts now load less code.

### Other changes

- **Quebec can hold a sovereignty referendum**
  In 2027 worlds, Quebec's Premier can now ask for a sovereignty referendum. If it passes and Canada's House of Commons consents, Quebec becomes its own country.
- **Contributing: world simulations are no longer a merge requirement**
  Balance and economy pull requests are judged on rules tests, turn-cost measurements and live numbers. A world simulation runs only when the maintainer asks for one.
- **Index listing petition fee uses the right currency**
  The suggested lobbying fee for an index listing petition is now 2% of market value for companies that report in a non-anchor currency.
- **Canada's data foundation lands for the 2027 world**
  Canada now has its provinces, parties and national figures authored for the 2027 world. It is not open to players yet.
- **Canada has a province map**
  Canada's map page now shows its thirteen provinces and territories, with party organization, Commons seats, Premiers, approval, lean, sectors and logistics views.
- **Canada opens to players in new 2027 worlds**
  New 2027 worlds now include Canada as a playable country alongside the United States.
- **Canada's founding seats are checked against its parties**
  A build check now confirms every seat in Canada's founding House of Commons belongs to a party the 2027 world actually creates.
- **Canada's government pages show Parliament Hill**
  Canada's Prime Minister's office and central bank pages now use photos of Parliament Hill and the Bank of Canada instead of the flag.
- **Canadian characters get their own events**
  Four new everyday events for Canadian characters from 2027: wildfire smoke, a trade permit between provinces, a northern housing visit and the town's old hockey arena.
- **Independence no longer assumes the United Kingdom**
  The steps that turn a region into a new country now work from any parent country, so Quebec can follow Scotland and Wales once its successor exists.
- **Independence speaks for each country it leaves**
  Independence bills and announcements now name the right parliament and country, and the handover knows how to turn Canada's Quebec into the eight regions of an independent Quebec.
- **Quebec gets a sovereignty mood**
  Quebec now carries a sovereignty mood that moves each turn, using the same rules as Scotland, Wales and Northern Ireland.
- **Quebec is ready to exist as its own country**
  The game now knows what an independent Quebec looks like, ready for the day a sovereignty referendum carries. No world contains it yet.
- **The referendum feed names a newly independent country**
  When a region becomes independent, the referendum feed now says "Quebec became an independent country" instead of showing a short code.
- **The 2027 world gets its own landing page**
  The sign-in page for a 2027 world now shows its own headlines, tour and country list instead of the 2023 copy.
- **Deleted fund holders no longer block rebalancing**
  Queued withdrawals whose units were already burned are cancelled when their holder no longer exists.
- **Fewer false alt-account matches for moderators**
  Moderator tools no longer link players because they use a common phone or browser, and a match on graphics hardware alone is now a low lead.
- **Old page addresses redirect straight away**
  Old links such as /parties and /politicians now go straight to the country page instead of loading a blank page first.
- **Quieter turn log for routine index fund holds**
  An index fund held for one turn while it pays out redemptions no longer writes a warning. A one-off repair check for records from the previous world no longer fails after the reset.
- **Faster data repair backups**
  Data repairs that touch many documents now back them up in batches, so a large repair fits in the gap between turns.
- **Maintainers can approve contributor PRs from a comment**
  Contributor pull requests that touch economy or CI files can now be approved with a maintainer comment, and promotion pull requests skip the contributor checks.
- **2027 world setup fixes**
  A fresh 2027 world now seats Reform UK's members of parliament under their own party, and the setup check no longer reports background countries as broken. Other start dates and the live world are unchanged.
- **Behind-the-scenes cleanup of unused code**
  Removed code that nothing in the game uses any more, so the project is smaller and easier to maintain. Nothing changes for players.
- **Births and life expectancy follow country trends in the 2027 world**
  In a new 2027 world, each country's birth rate follows its own path to 2050 and people live a little longer every year. Family policy can still push the birth rate up or down. Other start dates and existing worlds are unchanged.
- **Code comment guard**
  Contributor tooling only: a new check stops code comments from filling up with dates, ticket numbers and history. Nothing changes in the game.
- **Operators get a repair command for a turn held after a mid-economy restart**
  When a restart cuts off the company-profit step, the turn is held for repair. A new command checks what was saved and lets the turn finish safely.
- **A new world starts clean**
  A world reset now rebuilds starting data from scratch instead of patching the old world's records, and checks the new world for anything left behind before the first turn can run.
- **Automatic check that every game action needs a sign-in**
  A new automated check confirms that every action that changes the game requires the right sign-in, so a future change cannot quietly open one up. Nothing changes for players.
- **Behind-the-scenes split of large turn and corporation files**
  Twelve of the largest turn, bond, banking and corporation files were split into smaller pieces so they stay easy to maintain. Nothing changes for players.
- **Behind-the-scenes tidy-up of screen code**
  Merged duplicated screen code and removed branches that could never run, so the project is smaller and easier to maintain. Nothing changes for players.
- **Bill page no longer shows Expired on open votes**
  Chamber vote countdowns on the bill page now follow turns, so a vote that is still open no longer reads Expired when turns run late.
- **Capacity economy rules moved out of constants**
  Contributor tooling only: the capacity pricing and build cost formulas now live in a rules module, and a new check stops constants from gaining functions. Nothing changes in the game.
- **Code comment cleanup**
  Behind-the-scenes tidy-up of explanatory notes in the game code. Nothing in the game changes.
- **Code comment cleanup**
  Behind-the-scenes tidy-up of explanatory notes in the game code. Nothing changes for players.
- **Code comment cleanup, part three**
  Behind-the-scenes tidy-up of explanatory notes in the game code. Nothing changes for players.
- **Country code comments tidied**
  Behind-the-scenes cleanup of explanatory notes in the country modules. Nothing changes in the game.
- **Fifty-four new American events for the 2027 world**
  In a 2027 world, American players now see a large set of new events about Congress, courts, states, industry, disasters and culture, plus regional stories from the Gulf coast to Alaska. Other start dates are unchanged.
- **Five busy endpoints split into focused modules**
  Commodity details, cabinet briefings, cabinet bills, caucus whips and site search now run from their own modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **Internal type-safety cleanup**
  Behind-the-scenes cleanup of how turn processing reads data. Nothing changes for players.
- **Internal type-safety cleanup, part 2**
  More behind-the-scenes cleanup of share trading, corporation turn handling and market share code. Nothing changes for players.
- **Late-game years are covered by a safety check**
  The game now has an automatic check that years after 2050 are labelled and handled like any other year. No player-facing behaviour changes.
- **Make regional referendum campaigns easier to find**
  Active Independence and Reunification campaigns now have a prominent callout and View campaign button above the relevant region's Overview and other tabs.
- **New worlds only start from data written for their start date**
  Starting a new world now checks that every piece of opening data was written for that start date. If anything is missing, copied from another start date or out of scale for that era's economy, the world does not start and the admin sees the full list. Running worlds are unchanged.
- **Page and route cleanup**
  Behind-the-scenes tidy of a few pages and API routes: dead code and an unused database lookup removed. Nothing changes in how the game plays or looks.
- **Shared code for repeated screens and lists**
  Folded copies of the same code into shared pieces across navigation menus, approval, election, legislature, chart and staff screens. Nothing changes for players.
- **Shared code for staff and account requests**
  Requests that the game handled with copied code, such as removing a linked sign-in, uploading a screenshot and answering an offer, now share one version. Nothing changes for players.
- **Sign-in fixes for Discord and Google**
  New Discord accounts now get the same network check as Google and Apple sign-ups, and a failed Discord or Google sign-in now shows the normal error page instead of failing silently.
- **Sign-in, character creation and poll endpoints split into focused modules**
  Discord, Google and Apple sign-in now share one flow, and character creation, polls and the regional economy view run from their own modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **Source comment cleanup for pages and components**
  Behind-the-scenes tidy of explanatory notes in the page, API route and component code. Nothing in the game changes.
- **Starting data checked before a world is created**
  Two large starting data tables, background country political scores and voter group weights, are now stored as checked data files. Nothing changes in play.
- **Stricter checks before a world reset**
  A world reset now refuses to start if any state in a playable country is missing its own GDP or population. No player-facing change in the live game.
- **Turn and market code notes tidied**
  Behind-the-scenes cleanup of the explanatory notes in the turn, market, settlement, ledger and economy code. Nothing in the game changes.
- **United Kingdom code consolidated and server settings centralised**
  Behind-the-scenes cleanup. The United Kingdom's game rules now live in one place, and server settings are read through a single module. Nothing changes in the game.
- **Browser tests now run every night**
  The existing Playwright browser tests had never run in CI. A nightly workflow now builds the app against a throwaway database and runs them without blocking merges.
- **Commodity and currency rules moved out of constants**
  Contributor tooling only: the commodity market formulas and the exchange rate lookups now live in rules modules. Nothing changes in the game.
- **Corporation, command economy and tech tree rules moved out of constants**
  Contributor tooling only: the corporation margin and growth formulas, the command economy formulas and the tech tree selectors now live in rules modules. Nothing changes in the game.
- **Country, state, metric and sector strategy rules moved out of constants**
  Contributor tooling only: the country config selectors, government type predicates, US state seat selectors, metric lookups and sector strategy formulas now live in rules modules. Nothing changes in the game.
- **Guard against new voter archetype code**
  A repository check now fails when new code reintroduces voter archetypes. Voters are described by census groups only. No change to the game itself.
- **Internal type-safety cleanup, part 3**
  More behind-the-scenes cleanup of country merges, settlement and military lookups. Nothing changes for players.
- **Lean map breakdown shows census groups in every country**
  Clicking a region on the lean map now lists its census groups (age, income, region of origin and so on) with their share, turnout and lean, in every country instead of only the United States.
- **Lighter automated check memory use**
  The shared test helper that fakes the database now uses far less memory, so large automated checks stop running out of it. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Map lean reads the census electorate**
  When a region has no stored political lean, the map now works it out from the region's census groups instead of an older stored voter-group table.
- **Military model, cabinet estates and infrastructure, exchange and seat rules moved out of constants**
  Contributor tooling only: the unit power formulas, estate and infrastructure lookups, exchange registry lookups and historical seat selectors now live in rules modules. Nothing changes in the game.
- **More reliable automated checks**
  Two automated checks that sometimes failed for no real reason now run steadily. The game itself is unchanged.
- **New worlds start with the latest AI politicians**
  Newly created worlds now start with AI-run countries on the same politician behaviour the live world already uses. Existing worlds are unchanged.
- **Resets no longer stall on a false leftover alarm**
  The check that looks for leftovers after a world reset wrongly flagged three sets of seeded data as old, which would have kept a fresh world paused. It now judges them correctly.
- **Safety check before the 2027 world opens**
  A read-only check now runs against a database before and after the 2027 reset, so a broken start is caught before players see it. Nothing changes in running worlds.
- **Seceded regions use their own census**
  When Scotland or Wales secedes, each new region keeps its own authored population make-up instead of a split layered on the old national voter groups.
- **Seed and simulation tooling uses voter group wording**
  Internal seed, audit and simulation scripts no longer call voter groups archetypes. No change to the game itself.
- **Status bar, fund redemption, relocation and executive endpoints split into focused modules**
  The status bar, fund redemption, corporate relocation, executive overview, party whip leadership and Discord support endpoints now run from their own modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **Supreme Court vacancies fill without a player President**
  Empty Supreme Court seats now get a nomination and a Senate vote when the presidency is held by a computer-run politician or is vacant, and UK judicial review rulings now follow the governing party and only run where the UK exists.
- **admin code reads country rules from data tables**
  Behind the scenes, admin logic that was specific to one country now reads from a shared table instead of checking country codes in the code. Nothing changes in play.
- **API route code reads country rules from data tables**
  Behind the scenes, API route logic that was specific to one country now reads from a shared table instead of checking country codes in the code. Nothing changes in play.
- **Country constitutional and election steps run from one ordered list**
  The per-country constitutional, electoral and transition steps for Bulgaria, Romania, Russia, Hungary and the federations now live in one ordered list instead of being spread through the turn code. Nothing changes in play.
- **Country-only features are listed in one table**
  Which countries have referendums, party conferences, recall petitions, the Supreme Court docket and similar features is now read from a single list. Nothing changes for players.
- **Enacted UK referendums can finish conversion automatically**
  Separately settled Defence and Intelligence accounts no longer block Scotland and Wales from becoming independent, allowing pending UK referendum conversions to retry after parliamentary consent.
- **Law book seeding cleanup**
  Behind the scenes cleanup of how new worlds are set up with laws. Nothing changes for players.
- **page and component code reads country rules from data tables**
  Behind the scenes, page and component logic that was specific to one country now reads from a shared table instead of checking country codes in the code. Nothing changes in play.
- **Referendum conversions recover slates and construction safely**
  Independence consolidates colliding recruitment slates without dropping their candidates. Conversion refunds include construction owned by incoming Northern Ireland MPs' corporations, preserve completed secured plants and their loans, and reverse only construction already delivered. A blocked conversion now reports its reason and retries without preventing other regions from converting.
- **region banner and layer-1 model lookup code reads country rules from data tables**
  Behind the scenes, region banner and layer-1 model lookup logic that was specific to one country now reads from a shared table instead of checking country codes in the code. Nothing changes in play.
- **Retirement age now moves the dependency ratio, and the 2027 world opens with a realistic oldest age group**
  The dependency ratio follows the retirement age you set, and the 2027 world no longer opens with millions of people over 90.
- **seed and reset code reads country rules from data tables**
  Behind the scenes, seed and reset logic that was specific to one country now reads from a shared table instead of checking country codes in the code. Nothing changes in play.
- **shared library code reads country rules from data tables**
  Behind the scenes, shared library logic that was specific to one country now reads from a shared table instead of checking country codes in the code. Nothing changes in play.
- **turn and simulation code reads country rules from data tables**
  Behind the scenes, turn and simulation logic that was specific to one country now reads from a shared table instead of checking country codes in the code. Nothing changes in play.
- **A build check now blocks any new direct database access**
  The repository check that guards how code reaches stored game records now covers every record type, not a short list. No change to the game itself.
- **A cyber stand-down no longer shields the attacker**
  After a stand-down, the country that was attacked can retaliate once the attacker's own cooldown would have ended, instead of waiting out the full 72 turn truce.
- **A failed emergency aid pledge gives the money back**
  If filing the appropriation bill for an aid pledge fails, the treasury is refunded, the recovery effects are reversed and the pledge slot is freed.
- **A passed emergency bill waits for funds instead of vanishing**
  If the treasury cannot pay for a passed crisis emergency bill, the response is tried again next turn instead of being lost.
- **A script now prints the 1.20 code health numbers**
  Developer tooling only. One command prints the code size, duplication and type-safety counts used to track the 1.20 clean-up.
- **A turn interrupted by the update finishes without repeating steps**
  If the server updates while a turn is half finished, the new release recognises the steps the old one already ran, even where it groups them differently, and does not run them a second time.
- **A world reset leaves no old law types behind**
  Resetting a world now removes saved electorate priorities that point at laws from the old catalogue, and the post-reset check fails if any old law type survives.
- **Accounts, settings, metrics and logs get shared front doors**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of user accounts, game settings, state metrics, laws, cabinets, notifications, logs and similar records now goes through shared modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **Architecture check accepts the approval field move**
  The check that keeps old voter group wording out of the code now knows that the one-time move of saved approvals has to name the old saved field.
- **Bank and ledger records get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of loan, savings, bank movement and ledger records now goes through shared modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **Campaign cost check lists Quebec as waiting for its baseline**
  The automated check on campaign cost baselines now knows Quebec has no baseline row yet, like Canada. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Canada is back on the world map before 2027**
  Worlds set before 2027 show Canada on the world map again as a background economy you can inspect, as they did before Canada became playable in 2027.
- **Canada only appears in worlds started with it**
  Worlds that were already running before Canada was added carry on exactly as before, without a Canada they never set up.
- **Canada shares the Crown with the United Kingdom**
  Canada's monarch is the United Kingdom's monarch, as in real life, rather than a second separate crown. Canada is not playable yet.
- **Canada's bill phase is a known turn step**
  Canada's national bill step is registered with the other countries' bill steps, so the release builds cleanly.
- **Canada's executive titles and cyber law live with Canada**
  Canada's Premier titles, Quebec's Prefect titles and Canada's cyber security law are now kept with each country's own rules. No change for players.
- **Canada's regional statistics build without an unchecked type cast, with no player-visible change**
  The code that combines Canada's national baseline with each province's own figures now checks its data shape instead of forcing it. Every province's numbers are unchanged.
- **Canada's security act now sets its cyber defence**
  Canada had no law that changed its resilience to cyber attack. The Security Intelligence and Cyber Act now does.
- **Canada's voters**
  Canada now has its own voter groups, so policy and approval will reflect who lives in each province and territory once the country opens.
- **Canadian Premiers now count as regional executives**
  Provinces and territories now recognise their seated Premier, and an independent Quebec its Prefect, for party organisation and the region page.
- **Checks follow Germany, Japan and China into the law book**
  Automated checks that used Germany, Japan or China as examples of countries outside the law book now use France, and check the three countries on the path they actually use. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Code formatting cleanup, with no player-visible change**
  Two dozen files added during 1.20 were reformatted to the repository's code style. Nothing in a running game changes.
- **Code formatting pass for the 1.20 release**
  Source files changed during the 1.20 work are formatted to the project style so the release passes its automated checks. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Corporate sector records get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of corporate sector records now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Country data check skips database collection handles**
  The automated check that keeps each country's data in its own folder no longer mistakes database collection handles for country data. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Country display settings are typed as they are stored, with no player-visible change**
  The per-country display files now say in their types that an era can clear a setting, instead of hiding the difference behind a forced type. Nothing in a running game changes.
- **Country names, chambers and office titles now live in each country's folder**
  No visible change. The shared country display data was one 5,000 line file and is now split into each country's own folder.
- **Country order check includes Canada**
  The automated check on the registered country order now includes Canada. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Cyber news names countries in full**
  News posts and tension events about cyber operations used country codes. They now use country names.
- **Elected office records get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of elected office records now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Election records get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of election records now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Election, bill, campaign and government records get shared front doors**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of election candidate, vote tally, bill, campaign, party organisation, crisis, coalition and similar records now goes through shared modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **Formatting fix for two page tests**
  Two tests for the Canada and Quebec parliament and cabinet pages now follow the code formatter. No change for players.
- **Fund, order, budget and exchange rate records get shared front doors**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of index fund, share order, currency order, state budget, imperial character and exchange rate records now goes through shared modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **Game links can open the iPhone and iPad app**
  The website now tells iPhones and iPads that the A House Divided app may open its game pages, so links shared in Discord or email can open straight in the app.
- **Getting started in Canada**
  New Canadian characters get a getting-started checklist and welcome letter that point at their own province and Parliament and show amounts in Canadian dollars. Checks now keep it that way.
- **Hidden country check covers Quebec**
  The automated check that keeps not-yet-formed countries out of country pickers now covers Quebec. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Historical seat rosters now sit in each country's folder**
  No change in play. The starting seat lists for past eras moved out of one 4,000 line file into the folder of the country they belong to.
- **Independence promotion checks its parent country**
  When a region becomes independent, the step that splits its parent's opening records now runs only for parents that have those records. The game behaves the same.
- **Keep successor-country metrics usable after independence**
  National metrics created during independence remain readable after database storage, preventing approval and corporation processing from rejecting them. Existing affected records are normalized during their next metric refresh.
- **Nearly every game record now has a shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, reads and writes of bargaining, caucuses, share offers, regional budgets, leadership elections, reset records and about 180 other record types now go through shared modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **Non-player politician records get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of computer-controlled politician records now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Official referendum campaigns use the right country's Political Strength**
  Party officers running an official ground game in a Quebec referendum now spend Canadian Political Strength instead of failing against a UK lookup.
- **Old-law compatibility tables are marked as local-save only**
  The tables that keep old saved worlds working are now documented as being for local singleplayer saves, which are never reset.
- **Player character records get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of player character records now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Political party records get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of political party records now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Preserve deployment build diagnostics**
- **Prime minister and head of state appointment lists load again**
  A code change during 1.20 testing broke the candidate lists for appointing a prime minister or head of state. They load again.
- **Quebec's petition names the Premier**
  The Discord announcement of a Quebec independence petition now says the Premier of Quebec instead of the First Minister.
- **Records addressed by name constants get shared front doors too**
  Behind the scenes, about 870 more reads and writes of federation, election system, advertising, repair-tool and other records now go through shared modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **Regional metric texture for 1953 boards is built from typed seed data, with no player-visible change**
  The code that derives regional variation for 1953 boards now reads the regional seed data with its real types instead of forcing them. The derived numbers are unchanged.
- **Release checks count Japan's law book**
  Automated checks that count Japan's laws now expect the law book, and several release notes use valid labels. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Release checks understand a country that starts in 2027**
  Automated checks now judge Canada against the 2027 world it lives in, and check Canada's voter regions for full coverage. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Release checks updated for Canada as a player country**
  Automated checks now expect Canada among the 2027 player countries and the Canadian dollar as Canada's currency. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Seat and region tables move into each country's folder**
  Developer tooling only. The seat counts, electoral votes and region names for the US, UK, Russia, Nigeria, China and Germany now live beside each country's other data.
- **Shorter code comments on new 1.20 modules, with no player-visible change**
  Long or history-style code comments added during 1.20 were rewritten to say why the code works the way it does. Nothing in a running game changes.
- **Shorter comments on Canada's data files, with no player-visible change**
  Long comments on Canada's voter, metric and census data were shortened, and the comment check now follows seat and budget tables to their new country folders. Nothing in a running game changes.
- **Smaller admin bundle for the law type editor**
  The law type editor now loads its list of voter groups from the server instead of building it in the browser, which kept every country's voter model out of the page download.
- **Sovereignty copy names come from the sovereignty rules**
  Internal tidy-up with no change for players: the names of the regional and national governments in devolution copy now come from the same table as the rest of the sovereignty rules.
- **Starting budgets for more eras now live in each country's folder**
  Developer tooling only. The authored 1991, 1999, 2007, 2023 and 2027 national budgets moved out of one very large file into per-country files.
- **State records get one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of the state records now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Structured error logging in country turn phases, with no player-visible change**
  Failures in the per-country election and bill steps, and in the tension event for a foreign nationalization, are now logged through the shared error logger. Nothing in a running game changes.
- **Successor country profile check reads live regional GDP**
  The automated check that renders a Scottish, Welsh or Northern Irish profile now supplies the regional GDP the page prices campaign money from. The game itself is unchanged.
- **The economy-only reset refuses a world still on the old law book**
  The economy-only reset keeps laws, so it now stops and says so when any kept law, bill or saved priority still names a retired law type.
- **The remaining game records get shared front doors**
  Behind the scenes, reads and writes of achievements, news, mail, party records, roadmap items, API keys and well over a hundred other smaller record types now go through shared modules. Nothing changes in play.
- **The world state record gets one shared front door**
  Behind the scenes, every read and write of the world state record now goes through one shared module. Nothing changes in play.
- **Treasury check knows about the failed aid pledge refund**
  The check that lists every place allowed to move national treasury money now includes the refund a failed aid pledge makes. No change for players.
- **Two unused law book leftovers removed**
  Behind the scenes, two values left unused by the law book merge are gone. Nothing changes in play.
- **Type fixes for the 1.20 release checks**
  Three type errors in the release branch are fixed, including the region adjacency check now covering Quebec. The game itself is unchanged.
- **Type fixes for the political metrics compare view and update notices**
  The political metrics compare view now starts with every playable country in the one law book selected, including Germany, Japan and China. Two other type errors found by the full code check are fixed.
- **Type fixes in the reset law scan**
  The scan that finds retired laws before a reset now passes the type check. No change for players.
- **Typed database reads for basing, region transfers and wealth, with no player-visible change**
  Three database reads added during 1.20 now go through the shared typed collection handles. Nothing in a running game changes.
- **US voter group table moves into the US folder**
  The table that converts older saved US voter group drift now lives with the rest of the US data. No change for players.
- **Yukon's legislature has 19 seats**
  The Yukon Legislative Assembly was set at 21 seats. It has 19, which brings the provincial and territorial total to 778.
- **Alt checks only count accounts that share one device fingerprint**
  The staff alt check raised its strongest alert from every account linked by any fingerprint, so two separate two-account coincidences looked like one shared device. It now escalates only when a single fingerprint is held by three or more accounts.

## Detailed release notes

### Cheaper goods find more buyers

#### What changed

- A household used to place one order per good at one price limit and buy a
  fixed number of units. When a seller asked less, the household bought the
  same amount and kept the change, so more supply could never sell more.
- Behind a new switch, off until it is turned on, each good's order becomes a
  short ladder: the old order stays on top, and lower prices add the extra
  units a household would buy at that price. Every step of a good shares the
  same budget, so cheaper prices buy more with the same money and never spend
  more than before. Essentials such as food and energy barely react;
  vehicles, electronics and consulting react strongly.
- The same switch shifts spending with income: richer regions put more of
  their budget into discretionary goods and less into essentials. Households
  also save more of their cash when interest rates are high and spend more when
  they are low.
- A lower step that does not buy is not counted as demand, so it never makes a
  good look shorter than it is.

### Quebec can hold a sovereignty referendum

#### What changed

- Once Quebec's Sovereignty Desire reaches 60, the Premier can request a referendum. Canada's Prime Minister decides whether to grant it, and the campaign runs like Scotland's or Wales's.
- If Yes wins and the House of Commons passes the independence bill, Quebec leaves Canada as a new country with eight regions, a 125-seat National Assembly and the Canadian dollar.
- The Laurentian Alliance moves to the new country with its members. Members of Canada-wide parties in Quebec become Independents.
- The sitting Premier leads Quebec as caretaker while the first National Assembly, Prefect and regional council elections run. After that the Assembly sits for four years and passes its own laws.
- The Canada and Referendums wiki pages explain the full process.

### Ask Keir answer length: Concise or Standard

#### What changed

- Pick Concise for a short answer of a few sentences, or Standard for the usual two or three short paragraphs.
- The drawer remembers your choice on that device. Standard is the default.

### Ask Keir recovers when the connection drops mid-answer

#### What changed

- When the answer stream drops or goes quiet, the research card says "Reconnecting..." and checks every few seconds for the finished answer, for up to two minutes. When it arrives it shows as normal.
- If the answer never turns up, you get a plain message and a Retry button instead of a card stuck on "Researching".

### Choose prices for supplies sent to your own sites

#### What changed

- Added an internal transfer-price selector in Internal supply settings. Free remains the default.
- At-cost uses the supplier's break-even production quote; market-price uses the posted ask and the existing selectable discount.
- Recorded matching income and charges per site while preserving company cash, total producer profit, physical deliveries, and external quotes.
- Displayed the allocations separately in sector accounts and excluded internal income from the outside-sale price average.

### Contributing: world simulations are no longer a merge requirement

#### What changed

- The contributor guide no longer asks for a simulation report before a balance change can merge.
- Balance changes still need an issue first, deterministic rules tests, and a turn-cost measurement when they touch the turn.

### Government buying no longer arrives in one burst

#### What changed

- When a government budget line could not buy because a good was short, part
  of the unspent money was saved for later turns, up to four turns of budget.
  The moment supply arrived the line could spend all of it at once, so a seller
  saw revenue jump several times over for a single turn and then fall back.
- Behind a new switch, off until it is turned on: a line now draws at most half
  a turn of its usual budget from those savings each turn. The rest stays in the
  public wallet for later turns, still capped at four turns of budget.
- No money is created or lost: saved budget only changes when it is spent.

### Market books record how much demand buyers could actually pay for

#### What changed

- Every country and commodity book now keeps three new counts: requested (a reserved order and its fallback counted once), funded (what the buyers' shared cash covers at their bid prices) and filled (what they received).
- Prices still read the same demand figure as before. These counts let us see how much of a shortage is orders nobody can pay for before any price rule changes.

### Prices approach their ceiling instead of stopping on it

#### What changed

- Most goods in shortage sat exactly on the price ceiling and moved together
  each turn, so the price said nothing about how short a good was.
- Behind a new switch, off until it is turned on: above 60% of the ceiling, the
  price a good is heading for is softened so it approaches the ceiling without
  reaching it, and the usual limit on how far a price moves in one turn applies
  after that. A deeper shortage still shows a higher price.
- When the switch is turned on, goods sitting on the ceiling ease down to about
  85% of it within about eight turns, all in the same turns.
- Separation is clearest between 60% of the ceiling and shortages about twice as
  deep as the ceiling; deeper shortages still publish close to the ceiling.

### Research breakthroughs no longer inflate mineral deposits without limit

#### What changed

- A breakthrough at a mine or well grows the region's deposit only when the deposit is what limits that operation, and only for the resource that is short. A well running freely gets the revenue boost but no new deposit.
- Each breakthrough still adds 1 to 15% of the current deposit, but a region's deposit for any resource now stops at three times its starting value.
- Before this, every breakthrough grew every resource the mine produced, compounding without a ceiling. Some regions had reached hundreds of times their starting deposits, so the deposit limit on new mines never applied there.
- Existing deposits are not changed by this update.

### The sector page says why output did not sell

#### What changed

- The sector page showed how much of an output sold and nothing else, so a CEO
  could not tell a price problem from a demand problem.
- Under "What goes in, what comes out", any output that went partly unsold last
  turn now gets one sentence naming the cause, from what the plants asked
  against what buyers in that market actually paid: a price above the market,
  buyers shared with other sellers at the same price, every buyer at that price
  already supplied, or no trade in that market at all.
- The figures add up every plant in the sector, not one plant's row.
- The company commodity table now says that net production is added up across
  every site and does not mean your own plants received it.

### Workers can earn a larger share of what plants make

#### What changed

- Plant wages paid workers about an eighth of the value plants add. Most of the
  rest stayed with companies as profit, so households, who only spend what they
  are paid, bought about a fifth of everything sold.
- Behind a new switch, off until it is turned on: each plant raises its wages
  toward a target share of the value it added last turn (60% by default).
- The raise never takes more than a set share (75% by default) of what the
  plant had left last turn after base wages and site costs, so a plant that
  made little or no profit pays its normal wages and no plant is pushed into a
  loss by the raise on last turn's numbers.
- Wages never fall below the normal payroll. The higher wages are paid to the
  households of the plant's state like any other wage.

### Minimum wage laws can set the wage floor

#### What changed

- Behind a new switch, off until it is turned on, the national minimum wage law
  in force sets each country's wage floor as a share of the median wage. Until
  now these laws only moved income and poverty figures.
- Each option maps to a floor. Laws that name an hourly rate use that rate over
  a present-day median wage, so today's statutory options sit near real-world
  levels: about 28% of the median in the United States, 45% in Japan, 55% in
  Germany and 60% in Ireland. China's and Nigeria's options use fixed shares
  between 27% and 60%. Abolishing the minimum wage removes the floor.
- Industries that pay less than the floor raise their wages to it. With today's
  industry pay levels a floor at or below 50% of the median lifts nobody; above
  that, agriculture is lifted first, then retail, logistics and entertainment.
- Changing or repealing the law moves the floor from the next turn.
- The floor also feeds union pressure, as it already did: a weak floor adds a
  little to unionisation, a strong one takes a little away.
- When the switch is on, the law picker states the floor each option sets.
- A minimum wage set by an administrator still takes priority over the law.

### Wages can follow the local job market

#### What changed

- Behind a new switch, off until it is turned on, the share of a plant's value
  added that goes to wages depends on unemployment in its state instead of one
  fixed figure. Where unemployment is at or below 4.5%, plants aim for 55% of
  value added; at 12% or more they aim for 30%; in between the aim moves
  smoothly.
- Wages are sticky. A plant's wage share moves at most 3 points of value added
  a turn, so a jump in unemployment, or turning the switch on, walks wages to
  their new level over several turns.
- The same limits as before still apply: the raise never takes more than a set
  share of what the plant had left last turn, and wages never fall below the
  normal payroll.
- With the switch on, each country's wage growth, which feeds inflation and the
  income tax base, comes from its states' job markets: tight markets add growth,
  slack ones take it away, and wages that are still rising or falling add a
  capped amount on top. Countries without national figures no longer sit on a
  flat 3% wage growth.

### A shorter state panel in Campaign mode

#### What changed

- In Campaign mode the state panel starts with Your campaign here and folds Travel (or Camping), Operations, Canvassing and Field offices behind their labels. Tap a label to open it. Choosing a field-office colouring opens the Field offices section.
- On a desktop US race the map is the way into canvassing, so the Canvassing panel no longer repeats under the map. Phones and races without the map keep it where it was.
- Picking Field offices: lean or opportunity with a state open on a locked map now unlocks the map and zooms into that state, as the Open a field office link already did.
- The button on the race page that opens your campaign now reads Open campaign. The wiki and in-game guides point to the Campaign switch on the race page instead of the old Campaign Manager page.

#### Developer notes

- `CollapsibleSection` (`src/components/campaign`) wraps each section in `CampaignStateActions` and `FieldOfficeStatePanel`. The body is not mounted while folded, and `openWhen` opens it on a rising edge.
- `CampaignDeskView` hides the desk canvassing panel from `lg` up when `stateActionsOnMap` is set.
- Removed `officeCountyIds`, which the field-office map no longer used.

### Act on a state from the presidential map

#### What changed

- On a US presidential race, Campaign mode now shows the map above the campaign desk. It opens on the Your campaign colouring, so the states where your ticket has presence stand out.
- Click a state to open its panel. If you are the candidate you can travel there in the general election or camp there in the primary, with the cost shown on the button. The home-state surge appears in your home state's panel. In the primary you can also attack a rival in that state and see what is live there in both directions.
- Canvassing appears in the panel of the state you can canvass in.
- Campaign managers and the running mate now get the same Your campaign here block the candidate has had: the ticket's presence level in that state. Building presence stays with the candidate, and the field office link is shown to people who can manage the campaign.
- Each control shows only to people who could already use it. Viewing a rival's campaign, or one you are not on, shows the state's public overview and nothing else.
- On desktop the map is now the way into these actions, so the separate state operations list and the travel and camping panel no longer repeat beside the desk. Phones and races outside the US keep their compact lists.
- Old links with a state in them, such as the field office shortcut, open that state's panel on the map.

#### Developer notes

- `useCampaignDesk` (`src/components/campaign/useCampaignDesk.ts`) now loads the campaign, the viewer's resources, the state operations hub and the canvass eligibility once. `CampaignWorkspace` and the map's state panel read the same copy, so an action on the map shows in the desk panes and the other way round.
- `CampaignStateActions` renders the panel extras through the map's `panelExtra`. Which sections appear is decided by `stateActionSections` in `src/lib/campaignManager/mapActions.ts`, from data the server only sends to people who may act: the state presence payload and the operations hub are the candidate's alone, canvassing follows the desk's manage access.
- `TravelControls`, `PrimaryCampaignControls` and `StatePresencePanel` take an optional `focusState` for the one-click move, and `StateOperationsHere` is the per-state slice of the operations section.
- `useMyCampaign` takes the ticket's candidate and whether the viewer is that candidate. For anyone else it reads the ticket's levels from the public race-wide presence list and offers no build action. No server rule or route changed.
- The stage map takes `inPage` so it starts locked inside the scrolling Campaign mode page.

### Campaign managers can build Campaign Presence

#### What changed

- Campaign managers can now build Campaign Presence for their ticket, paid from the campaign's funds and actions as before. Open the race in Campaign mode, click a state on the map and use the build button in Your campaign here.
- Prices, the one build per state per turn limit and what presence is worth are unchanged. The level is the candidate's, and it counts for the ticket as it always has.
- The running mate and everyone outside the campaign still cannot build, and a campaign that has been eliminated or suspended cannot be built for.

#### Developer notes

- `POST /api/political-operations/state-org/build` takes an optional `campaignId`. Without it nothing changes. With it, `resolvePresenceSubject` (`src/lib/campaigns/statePresenceAccess.ts`) loads the campaign, checks the caller is the candidate's own account or a manager (`isCampaignManagerUser`), refuses archived and suspended campaigns and NPP tickets, and returns the candidate. The row is written under the candidate, priced in the candidate's home currency and debited from the campaign through the same guarded update.
- A manager's build also writes an `activityLog` game action (`state_org_build`) naming the manager, best effort. `Campaign.activityHistory` has no actor field and is capped, so presence builds do not go there.
- `GET /api/political-operations/state-org/list` takes the same optional `campaignId` and returns the candidate's rows for a manager.
- The map reads `presenceBuildAccess` (`src/lib/campaignManager/mapActions.ts`) to decide between the candidate's own list, the campaign-named list for a manager, and no build.

### Field offices now live on the presidential map

#### What changed

- On a US presidential race in Campaign mode the map has two new colour-by views: Field offices: lean and Field offices: opportunity. Lean shows how each county leans. Opportunity shows where a new office would add the most turnout, and it is offered only to people who can manage the campaign, as before. Gold states hold offices, and counties with one of the campaign's offices carry a pin.
- Click a county in either view to see its lean, its share of the state, and for managers what a new office there is worth, with the Open office button and the cost. A county that already has an office shows Close office.
- The state panel lists the campaign's offices in that state with their strength and a Close button for people who can manage them.
- Open a field office in this state, in the Your campaign here block, now flies the map into that state's counties in the field-office view instead of jumping to the Field offices tab. Old links with tab=field and a state open that state on the map in the same view.
- The Field offices tab keeps its totals, upkeep, the list of current offices and the explanation, and points at the map instead of drawing a second county map.
- On a phone the map stays locked by default, so the state panel lists the best counties for a new office and you can open one from there.
- Anyone viewing a rival's campaign sees the same thing the Field offices tab showed them before: where the offices are, and the lean view, with no costs or values.

#### Developer notes

- The field-office views are `fieldLean` and `fieldValue` in `MapDataView`. County rows come from the existing `/api/campaigns/[id]/field-offices` route through `useFieldOfficeLayer`, which the map reads for the open state only. Fill helpers moved from `fieldOfficeMapMath.ts` to `presMap/fieldOfficeMath.ts` next to the other data views, with the pure view and ranking rules.
- `PresidentialMap` takes a `fieldOffices` config from `useRaceMapCampaign` (Campaign mode, US races with field offices). Access follows the route: managers get opportunity and costs, everyone else lean and office locations. No server rule changed.
- `MyCampaignStateBlock` reaches the map through `FieldOfficeMapProvider`, so the link flies the map into the state and falls back to the campaign link where there is no map.
- `FieldOfficesSection` no longer draws the county map. It takes `mapOnPage`, `mapHref` and `reloadKey`, and reloads when the campaign's office count changes.

### Index listing petition fee uses the right currency

#### What changed

- A company's market value was read in its home currency as if it were anchor currency, so the suggested lobbying fee and the size check could be far too high for companies such as those reporting in yen.
- Market value is now converted to anchor currency first. Anchor-currency companies are unchanged.

### Natural Gas Focus extraction strategy

#### What changed

- New extraction strategy: **Natural Gas Focus**. It makes natural gas first (0.58) and crude oil as a byproduct (0.32), the reverse of Oil & Gas.
- It uses the same inputs as Oil & Gas and earns the same margin at normal prices, so pick whichever of the two fuels your market is short of.
- AI companies that switch mines to target a natural gas shortage now use Natural Gas Focus. They still use Oil & Gas for oil shortages.
- Suggested by a player (S#422).

### Own-site supply wording on sector pages

#### What changed

- The money panel no longer says own-site supplies were free when they were charged to those sites.
- The outputs row shows what the own-site share is worth next to the sold share.

### Sites with construction loans can now change hands

#### What changed

- A site financed by a secured construction loan used to block almost every
  ownership change until the loan was fully repaid, which could take up to 120
  turns.
- Agreed acquisitions, hostile takeovers and subsidiary spin-offs now go
  through. The loan moves with the site and the new owner becomes the borrower,
  repaying it from its own cash each turn. The bank keeps its loan and its claim
  on the site.
- Nationalizing a sector or a whole corporation, splitting off or merging back a
  National Corporation, and reabsorbing an unsold privatization work the same
  way: the National Corporation that receives the site takes on its loan.
- Selling a site on the market already worked and still does: the buyer's
  payment repays the bank first and the seller gets the rest.
- Because the receiver now repays the bank, the price is net of the loan. State
  compensation for a taken site or corporation subtracts the construction loan
  principal the state takes on, never going below zero. The minimum price for an
  acquisition, and the price floor for a hostile takeover or a take-private
  buyout, now subtract the bank loans the company owes.
- When an acquisition or hostile takeover absorbs a company, its ordinary bank
  loans now become the acquirer's debt. Before, those loans were left pointing
  at a company that no longer existed and slid into default. A loan request
  still waiting on the bank, or a loan in a different currency from the
  acquirer's cash, blocks the deal until it is settled.
- Nationalizing a whole corporation works the same way: its ordinary bank loans
  pass to the country's National Corporation, and shareholder compensation is
  net of them, never going below zero. A pending loan request, or a loan in a
  different currency from the National Corporation's cash, blocks the taking.

#### What still waits for the loan

- A financed site cannot be merged into another site the new owner already runs
  in the same market.
- The new owner must keep its cash in the loan's currency.
- A sector-wide nationalization still leaves financed sites in private hands,
  because it splits or folds sites into new records that cannot carry the loan.
- Dissolving a corporation, moving it to another currency, privatizing a
  financed site and merging countries still wait until the loan is repaid or
  the site is sold, because none of them leaves anyone to repay the bank.
- Sites whose loan is in default, still waiting on the bank, or in the middle of
  a payment, sale or refund also wait.

### The presidential race page now has a Campaign mode

#### What changed

- The presidential race page has a Race and Campaign switch beside the title, on desktop and on a phone. Race shows the map and scoreboard as before. Campaign shows the campaign desk for one ticket under the same title and ticker.
